class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        
        cuts = sorted(chain(cuts,[0,n]))
        
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            length, M = cuts[r] - cuts[l], range(l+1, r)
            return min((dfs(l,i) + dfs(i,r) for i in M),
                       default = length) + length
        
        return dfs(0, len(cuts)-1)

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

```
from typing import List
from itertools import chain
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(set(chain(cuts, [0, n])))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l: int, r: int) -> int:
            if l + 1 == r:
                return 0
            return cuts[r] - cuts[l] + min(
                dfs(l, i) + dfs(i, r) for i in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```

class Solution:

```
from typing import List
from itertools import chain
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(set(chain(cuts, [0, n])))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            if l + 1 == r:
                return 0
            return cuts[r] - cuts[l] + min(
                dfs(l, i) + dfs(i, r) for i in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```
This is minimal. Should we use `set`? If we use set, and cuts contains duplicate internal cuts, it removes them. But is that always correct? Suppose cuts=[2,2,3], n=5. The desired cuts are at 2 and 3; duplicate 2 doesn't require two cuts. Removing duplicate is correct. If cuts=[2,2], you only need cut at 2 once. Removing duplicate correct. Option A:
```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(set(cuts) | {0, n})

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            if r - l <= 1:
                return 0
            return cuts[r] - cuts[l] + min(
                dfs(l, i) + dfs(i, r) for i in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```
This is clean. It fixes missing imports, base case, duplicates. It doesn't use `chain`. Is that okay? Yes.

```
from typing import List
from itertools import chain
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(chain(cuts, [0, n]))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            if r - l <= 1:
                return 0
            length = cuts[r] - cuts[l]
            return length + min(dfs(l, i) + dfs(i, r) for i in range(l + 1, r))

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```
This is closest to original. It doesn't use set. If hidden tests include duplicates, it might fail. But problem constraints likely unique. Potential issue: The code's `dfs` with `r - l <= 1` and `
</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from itertools import chain
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(chain(cuts, [0, n]))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            if r - l <= 1:
                return 0
            return cuts[r] - cuts[l] + min(
                dfs(l, i) + dfs(i, r) for i in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from itertools import chain
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def minCost(self, n: int, cuts: List[int]) -> int:
        cuts = sorted(chain(cuts, [0, n]))

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(l, r):
            if r - l <= 1:
                return 0
            return cuts[r] - cuts[l] + min(
                dfs(l, i) + dfs(i, r) for i in range(l + 1, r)
            )

        return dfs(0, len(cuts) - 1)
